# Country profiles — Middle East and Central Asia

IMF World Economic Outlook, IMF Financial Soundness Indicators and credit-to-GDP gaps (BIS, or IMF Monetary and Financial Statistics for Kuwait, UAE, Qatar and Oman) for 32 economies in Middle East and Central Asia. Each country has its own section below. Built 2026-09-28 from github.com/Aknarik/country-data.

## Kuwait — credit-to-GDP ratio, Hodrick-Prescott trend and credit gap

Kuwait: Credit = claims on other sectors minus claims on public non-financial corporations (end of year); ratio = credit / nominal GDP x 100; trend: one-sided HP filter, lambda = 100,000, from 10 years after the series start. Gap = ratio minus trend, percentage points of GDP. Basel III countercyclical buffer guide: gap above 2 pp may signal a buffer build-up; above 10 pp the maximum buffer. Gap mini chart (2014 to 2025): ▄██▆▄▄▆▃▁▃▄▅ (min -22.5, max 22.2).

| Period | Credit-to-GDP ratio (% of GDP) | HP trend (% of GDP) | Gap (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2014 | 70.7 | 72.0 | -1.3 |
| 2015 | 102.4 | 80.2 | 22.2 |
| 2016 | 109.7 | 88.4 | 21.3 |
| 2017 | 102.3 | 93.3 | 9.0 |
| 2018 | 93.2 | 95.5 | -2.3 |
| 2019 | 94.8 | 97.5 | -2.7 |
| 2020 | 114.2 | 102.7 | 11.5 |
| 2021 | 91.3 | 103.0 | -11.7 |
| 2022 | 78.5 | 101.0 | -22.5 |
| 2023 | 88.9 | 100.9 | -12.0 |
| 2024 | 95.7 | 101.9 | -6.2 |
| 2025 | 104.4 | 104.0 | 0.4 |

## Oman — credit-to-GDP ratio, Hodrick-Prescott trend and credit gap

Oman: Credit = claims on other sectors minus claims on public non-financial corporations (end of year); ratio = credit / nominal GDP x 100; trend: one-sided HP filter, lambda = 100,000, from 10 years after the series start. Gap = ratio minus trend, percentage points of GDP. Basel III countercyclical buffer guide: gap above 2 pp may signal a buffer build-up; above 10 pp the maximum buffer. Gap mini chart (2013 to 2024): ▄▅▇█▇▅▅▇▄▁▂▃ (min -12.8, max 17.1).

| Period | Credit-to-GDP ratio (% of GDP) | HP trend (% of GDP) | Gap (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2013 | 40.7 | 39.6 | 1.1 |
| 2014 | 43.7 | 41.3 | 2.4 |
| 2015 | 61.4 | 46.9 | 14.6 |
| 2016 | 70.4 | 53.3 | 17.1 |
| 2017 | 69.4 | 58.3 | 11.1 |
| 2018 | 64.7 | 61.3 | 3.4 |
| 2019 | 69.0 | 64.6 | 4.4 |
| 2020 | 80.8 | 69.5 | 11.3 |
| 2021 | 72.1 | 72.0 | 0.0 |
| 2022 | 59.2 | 72.0 | -12.8 |
| 2023 | 63.8 | 72.6 | -8.7 |
| 2024 | 67.6 | 73.7 | -6.1 |

Source: International Monetary Fund, Monetary and Financial Statistics (dataset IMF.STA:MFS_DC; depository corporations' claims on other sectors less claims on public non-financial corporations) and World Economic Outlook (nominal GDP). https://data.imf.org

## Qatar — credit-to-GDP ratio, Hodrick-Prescott trend and credit gap

Qatar: Credit = claims on other sectors minus claims on public non-financial corporations (end of year); ratio = credit / nominal GDP x 100; trend: one-sided HP filter, lambda = 100,000, from 10 years after the series start. Gap = ratio minus trend, percentage points of GDP. Basel III countercyclical buffer guide: gap above 2 pp may signal a buffer build-up; above 10 pp the maximum buffer. Gap mini chart (2014 to 2025): ▂▄▅▄▃▅█▅▁▂▂▂ (min -5.7, max 45.4).

| Period | Credit-to-GDP ratio (% of GDP) | HP trend (% of GDP) | Gap (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|

| 2015 | 73.7 | 55.3 | 18.4 |
| 2016 | 83.5 | 63.2 | 20.3 |

| 2018 | 83.0 | 74.6 | 8.3 |
| 2019 | 103.1 | 82.6 | 20.4 |

| 2021 | 126.0 | 105.3 | 20.7 |
| 2022 | 103.0 | 108.7 | -5.7 |
| 2023 | 117.5 | 113.9 | 3.6 |
| 2024 | 121.3 | 118.9 | 2.4 |
| 2025 | 125.3 | 123.8 | 1.5 |

## United Arab Emirates — credit-to-GDP ratio, Hodrick-Prescott trend and credit gap

United Arab Emirates: Credit = claims on other sectors minus claims on public non-financial corporations (end of year); ratio = credit / nominal GDP x 100; trend: one-sided HP filter, lambda = 100,000, from 10 years after the series start. Gap = ratio minus trend, percentage points of GDP. Basel III countercyclical buffer guide: gap above 2 pp may signal a buffer build-up; above 10 pp the maximum buffer. Gap mini chart (2014 to 2025): ▃▇▇▅▄▄█▄▁▂▃▅ (min -19.8, max 1.5).

| Period | Credit-to-GDP ratio (% of GDP) | HP trend (% of GDP) | Gap (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2014 | 64.2 | 78.1 | -13.9 |
| 2015 | 77.7 | 80.3 | -2.7 |
| 2016 | 80.6 | 82.7 | -2.1 |
| 2017 | 76.9 | 83.7 | -6.8 |
| 2018 | 73.2 | 83.8 | -10.6 |
| 2019 | 74.4 | 84.0 | -9.6 |
| 2020 | 88.2 | 86.6 | 1.5 |
| 2021 | 75.6 | 86.6 | -11.0 |
| 2022 | 64.9 | 84.7 | -19.8 |
| 2023 | 67.6 | 83.5 | -15.8 |
| 2024 | 69.2 | 82.7 | -13.4 |
| 2025 | 74.2 | 82.7 | -8.4 |
